=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N mochi (rice cakes), arranged in ascending order of size.
The size of the i-th mochi (1\leq i\leq N) is A_i.
Given two mochi A and B, with sizes a and b respectively, you can make one kagamimochi (a stacked rice cake) by placing mochi A on top of mochi B if and only if a is at most half of b.
You are given Q integer pairs. Let (L_i, R_i) be the i-th pair (1\leq i\leq Q), and solve the following problem for each i:

Using only the R_i - L_i + 1 mochi from the L_i-th to the R_i-th, how many kagamimochi can you make simultaneously?  
More precisely, find the maximum non-negative integer K such that:

- Out of the R_i - L_i + 1 mochi from the L_i-th to the R_i-th, choose 2K mochi and form K pairs. For each pair, place one mochi on top of the other, to make K kagamimochi.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dotsc A_N
Q
L_1 R_1
L_2 R_2
\vdots
L_Q R_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line (1\leq i\leq Q) should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i \leq 10^9 \ (1 \leq i \leq N)
- A_i \leq A_{i+1} \ (1 \leq i < N)
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq L_i < R_i \leq N \ (1 \leq i \leq Q)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

11
1 1 2 3 4 4 7 10 11 12 20
5
2 5
3 8
7 11
1 2
1 11

Sample Output 1

2
3
1
0
5

The answers to each query are as follows. One possible way to make the kagamimochi is given for each query.

- The mochi sizes are (1, 2, 3, 4). You can make the two kagamimochi (1,3) and (2,4).
- The mochi sizes are (2, 3, 4, 4, 7, 10). You can make the three kagamimochi (2,4), (3,7), and (4,10).
- The mochi sizes are (7, 10, 11, 12, 20). You can make one kagamimochi (10,20).
- The mochi sizes are (1, 1). You cannot make any kagamimochi.
- The mochi sizes are (1, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4, 7, 10, 11, 12, 20). You can make five kagamimochi (1,2), (1,3), (4,10), (4,11), and (7,20).

Hence, print 2, 3, 1, 0, 5 in this order.

24
127 148 170 174 258 311 331 414 416 436 517 523 532 587 591 638 660 748 760 776 837 857 972 984
15
7 11
8 9
8 13
12 15
9 23
1 17
8 12
1 5
6 17
3 7
12 19
13 18
7 22
1 12
14 15

Sample Output 2

0
0
0
2
6
0
1
1
0
0
0
3
5
0

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

I think identity matching is optimal (if any matching, sorted matching works). This is because the bipartite graph is a "chain graph" (threshold) and the greedy matching that matches smallest top to smallest feasible bottom yields sorted order; if it fails, no matching. Then extreme condition proof:

Now, let's think if answer can be computed by binary search with RMQ but using `g[p]` as first index difference. We should prove condition exactly:
For interval [L,R] 0-index? Let's use 1-index in explanation but code 0-index.

Given K, top block positions L..L+K-1 (1-index). For each j, bottom position = R-K+j. Let p=L+j-1. Then bottom = p + (R-K+1 - L) = p + (R-L+1-K) = p + m - K. Let d=m-K. Need A[p+d] >= 2A[p]. This is equivalent to p+d >= nxt[p], i.e. d >= nxt[p]-p = g[p]. Thus condition for all p in top block. So max_g <= d. Good.

Now, binary search monotonic:

Let's write code:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    idx = 0
    N = data[idx]; idx += 1
    A = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N
    Q = data[idx]; idx += 1

    INF = N + 1
    g = [0] * N
    r = 0
    for p in range(N):
        th = A[p] * 2
        while r < N and A[r] < th:
            r += 1
        if r < N:
            g[p] = r - p
        else:
            g[p] = INF

    LOG = N.bit_length()
    st = [g]
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        span = 1 << (k - 1)
        length = N - (1 << k) + 1
        cur = [0] * length
        for i in range(length):
            a = prev[i]
            b = prev[i + span]
            cur[i] = a if a >= b else b
        st.append(cur)

    log = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        log[i] = log[i >> 1] + 1

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        L = data[idx]; R = data[idx+1]; idx += 2
        m = R - L + 1
        lo = 0
        hi = m // 2
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi + 1) >> 1
            k = log[mid]
            row = st[k]
            span = 1 << k
            left = L - 1
            right = L + mid - 2
            mx = row[left]
            v = row[right - span + 1]
            if v > mx:
                mx = v
            if mx <= m - mid:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid - 1
        out.append(str(lo))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Before final, let's revisit the proof of sorted matching identity one more time because if false, our whole solution fails. Let's try to find a counterexample systematically.

We have t sorted, b sorted. Suppose there exists permutation π with t_i <= b_{π(i)}/2. Does it imply t_i <= b_i/2 for all i? This is a known property for "Monge" or "noncrossing matching" in threshold graphs. Let's attempt a brute force search mentally for K=3.

Need pair 11 with >=22 -> b3=24, 12 with >=24 -> b3? only one >=24? b3=24, 12 needs >=24, yes but b3 used by 11? 11 needs >=22, could use b3=24; 12 needs >=24, no other. 1 can use b1=6 or b2=20. If 1 uses b1=6, 11 uses b3=24, 12 no. If 1 uses b2=20, 11 uses b3=24, 12 no. No matching. Good.
Try t=[1, 11, 23], b=[6, 22, 46]. Identity: 1<=3,11<=11,23<=23 valid.

```
import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    Q = int(input())
    # build g/st
    ...
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        L, R = map(int, input().split())
        ...
```
This assumes A line contains all N. The problem input format indeed has A_1 ... A_N on one line. AtCoder always follows. It is memory lower and avoids `data` list. Speed: `readline` 2e5 times for queries; okay. Building st before reading queries means input buffer may have unread data, no issue. I think this is acceptable and perhaps more memory-safe. However, if the A line is extremely long (2e5 numbers ~2MB), `readline` can read it. Yes.

But what if the input has line breaks in A due to formatting? The format says one line, but some judges may wrap? Usually not. To be robust, read split all. Competitive programming solutions often use read all to handle arbitrary whitespace. I'd lean read all for robustness. Memory still okay. Let's use read all.

Could combine: read all but not store queries? We can parse all anyway. Fine.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    idx = 0

    N = data[idx]
    idx += 1
    A = data[idx:idx + N]
    idx += N

    Q = data[idx]
    idx += 1

    INF = N + 1

    # g[i] = distance to the first position j such that A[j] >= 2 * A[i]
    g = [0] * N
    r = 0
    for i in range(N):
        need = A[i] * 2
        while r < N and A[r] < need:
            r += 1
        if r < N:
            g[i] = r - i
        else:
            g[i] = INF

    # Sparse table for range maximum of g
    LOG = N.bit_length()
    st = [g]
    for k in range(1, LOG):
        prev = st[-1]
        span = 1 << (k - 1)
        length = N - (1 << k) + 1
        cur = [0] * length
        for i in range(length):
            a = prev[i]
            b = prev[i + span]
            cur[i] = a if a >= b else b
        st.append(cur)

    # floor(log2(i))
    log = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(2, N + 1):
        log[i] = log[i >> 1] + 1

    out = []
    append = out.append

    for _ in range(Q):
        L = data[idx]
        R = data[idx + 1]
        idx += 2

        m = R - L + 1
        lo = 0
        hi = m // 2

        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi + 1) >> 1

            # Check whether K = mid is feasible.
            # Need max(g[L-1 ... L+mid-2]) <= m - mid.
            k = log[mid]
            row = st[k]
            span = 1 << k

            left = L - 1
            right = L + mid - 2

            mx = row[left]
            v = row[right - span + 1]
            if v > mx:
                mx = v

            if mx <= m - mid:
                lo = mid
            else:
                hi = mid - 1

        append(str(lo))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
